In this context, policy and the situation in international markets are very important. Poland is a major producer of food in the world, it is a country which is, if it can be said, structurally doomed to export. Without exports, Polish agriculture would simply choke. We produce a huge amount of products that we would not be able to absorb in the internal market. This concerns meat and its preparations, and this also concerns plant production. We are doing fairly well in international markets. We're looking for new markets all the time. I will present the data for the second half of last year and until April, i.e. the most up-to-date data, very briefly. As far as cereals are concerned, we exported 1.5 million t of wheat, i.e. 5% more than in the same period of the previous year, mainly to Saudi Arabia, Egypt and Germany. I want to pay particular attention to these first two countries. For us, the markets are primarily those countries that are looking for grain and in a sustainable way because of the climate of this grain, they will never have the right amount, i.e. the countries of northern Africa, the Gulf countries, in general the countries of that part of the world. The problem is that we don't have an efficient port to tranship seeds, including cereals. There are private quays that generally decide for themselves who to sell the grain to. That is why we have made a decision, she will be implemented this year, about launching a state quay for the handling of cereals and other agricultural seeds. This will solve the problem, because then organised groups of farmers, operators, large producers will have the opportunity to sell through this state port. Balance is favourable, 14% more than a year ago, so as far as grain is concerned, the situation is not the worst. Prices are, of course, higher than a year ago, but this is not only a matter of regulation in international markets, but also that we had less cereals. Last year Polish agriculture was affected by severe drought, so it had an impact. At the moment wheat is about 16% more expensive, rye even more than 20%, feed barley about 17%. This may be a delight to farmers, to those who are selling it, but we also need to take into account prices that can translate into food prices. The fruit and vegetables market is a difficult market. Last year, mainly ground vegetables due to a small number of irrigation systems turned out to be unreliable crops, we cleaned out considerably less ground vegetables, which translated into prices, among others. The mythical parsley you've been talking about. But you can't compare things that are incomparable. If I hear and see great noise in the media because of the increase in food prices and there is an example of imported cherries from Spain of 70 PLN per kg, it is simply unfair, because it is necessary to compare what will be from Polish production, from Polish orchards. As for the production of vegetables and fruit, this year is not entirely clear. The spring drought caused some crops to be sown. They will also produce less orchards, among others. because there have been frost-related losses in some varieties. But consumers also need to understand that as far as field production is concerned, this production is also dependent on nature and there may be differences and fluctuations in prices. The milk market is stable, exports are growing, prices remain decent. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u13	Thank you. Minister, please respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u14	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! First of all, I would like to point out that the first exploration drilling for oil and gas in the Ropica, Sękowa, Siera area began in 1860 and ended in 1988. Over a more than 120-year period of exploration of oil and natural gas resources, the region enjoyed a high and continuous interest in the oil industry. During this time almost 800 drilling holes were made available for exploration and distribution of hydrocarbons. As a result of the work carried out, the oil deposit of Sulphur - Sękowa - Ropica, whose mining resources were calculated at 209.8 thousand, was documented. a. The operation of the deposit was completed in December 2000. In the last two years of operation, the monthly oil extraction was around 3.8 t, i.e. Approximately 150 liters of oil a day. During the lifetime of the deposit 99.8% were extracted resources of extracted crude oil accumulated in this deposit. Given that the deposit was exhausted in 2001, the Minister of the Environment deleted the deposit from the records of the fossil deposits and in August 2018 issued a decision to terminate the concession No 75/92 for the extraction of crude oil from the deposit of Siera - Sękowa - Ropica Górna. With the date of expiry of the licence, the mining area and the mining area designated for the deposit in question was abolished. At the end of the operation of the deposit, the trader began to liquidate wells and surface infrastructure. The decommissioning of the well in which a mixture of deposit water, oil, natural gas, or Francis 1 was leaked, was already carried out in 1996 due to the loss of oil production. I would like to point out that, in accordance with the rules in force, it is an entrepreneur who carries out mining activities in a given area, he is obliged to liquidate the wells and, if unsuccessful decommissioning is found, he is obliged to take action to ensure that these wells are effectively disposed of. The liquidation consisted in cleaning the well to the bottom, i.e. up to a depth of 460 m, then a lumber stopper was made from underneath to a depth of 390 m, above which cement corks were made at a depth of 390-360 m and from 20 m to the surface of the site. The space between the cement corks was filled with a thick wash, and the place after the cut-off was marked with a concrete cocoon. In case of accidental cases in a depleted deposit after the end of operation there may be a slow process of displacement by deposit water of residual oil to the surface, resulting pressure increases may result in leakages in closed wells. Given the amount of drilling wells carried out and the amount of leaks from the closed holes, I think we can talk about the fact that the closed wells do not pose a threat to the surrounding inhabitants and the environment, and the situation we have been dealing with in Sękowa is extremely rare and is due to the unpredictable behaviour of the mountaineer. Therefore, if there are further similar situations, the procedure will have to be verified, the way in which the holes already used are secured. As a result, 205 m3 of the mixture was removed from the closed well of Franek, which began on 20 April 2019, mainly consisting of deposit water contaminated with oil from the well and traces of gas-saturated oil. I would also like to stress that the leak is ongoing and monitored by PGNiG's watchers 24/7, while the company has made efforts to secure the leak. I would also like to point out that drilling outside the mining area and the mining area, and in this case this is the situation that we have been dealing with under the applicable rules, is the property of the State Treasury, and their control and security are the responsibility of the competent voivode. I would also like to point out that in the case of Jękowa, actions by the PGNiG services that secured the site and started to remove the effects of the leak were quickly and efficiently taken. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u30	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! For some time now, the Ministry of Energy has explained issues concerning energy prices both in Poland and in Europe on the wholesale market and on the retail market in its various segments. I'm gonna try and put it back together synthetically. what is most important for a proper understanding of the functioning of the entire market. The first key element for the electricity market, which I would like to mention, is the way in which the transaction is concluded. That's what I'm talking about. Mr Sitarski asked in his speech. Electricity trading in Poland takes place on the stock market operated by the Freight Exchange of Energy, the balancing market operated by the transmission system operator, bilateral contracts between the manufacturer and the trading company, as well as in direct contracts between the manufacturer and the connected end-user where the so-called end-user is used. the direct line. The transaction structure in percentage terms is as follows: approx. 68% all sales represent sales in bilateral contracts, about 24% are sales on the stock market, about 6% - sales on the balancing market, around 2% constitutes direct sales to electricity consumers. The situation in the way electricity is sold significantly changed the law passed last year - in November 2018. It introduced an increased stock exchange obligation. This regulation has allowed electricity prices to be marketed in the wholesale market as from 2019. The effects of implementing this law will be known after the end of 2019, when all the statistics will be collected and all the contracts completed. The Act obliged manufacturers to sell all the energy generated by the stock exchange. Another key element is the issue of electricity prices on the wholesale market. I will start by comparing energy prices on the wholesale market in the European Union to those in selected countries outside the European Union. The international comparison shows that the real wholesale prices of electricity in the Union are higher than in the United States, Canada or Russia, but lower than in China, Japan, Brazil and Turkey. These are the main economic directions that we compare with when we talk about products sold on global markets. Comparing electricity prices on the wholesale market in Poland to prices in other countries of the European Union, it can be said that our market is quite good. According to data published by the European Commission's Energy Directorate in 2018, the average energy price on the wholesale market in Poland was EUR 57,6 per MWh. In a number of EU countries in 2018, the price of electricity was higher than in Poland. In France, the price was higher by EUR 5.4, in Italy by EUR 11. There are only a few countries where the price was lower, but the differences are minor. In Germany, the wholesale market price was EUR 52,7 per MWh, i.e. about EUR 5 less than in Poland. However, we are talking about a wholesale price which is not the same as the end-user's: whether it's at the retail customer's, or at a large customer's consumption of large amounts of energy. Between the wholesale price and the end-user price, there is a fundamental difference - there are also different charges, taxes or policy financing elements. As a result, households in Poland pay less than households in Germany, although the wholesale price in Germany is lower than the wholesale price in Poland. While remaining for a while at wholesale electricity prices in Europe, I would like to stress that in 2018 electricity prices were rising across Europe on stock exchanges. This situation was not only about Poland, but about Europe as a whole. Among the main price-setting factors affecting electricity prices, the costs of CO2 allowances and fuel costs should be identified. The obligation to purchase emission allowances is the main element of EU climate policy. In 2017, the average price of CO2 allowances was EUR 5 per tonne, reaching a maximum value of EUR 25 I would also like to draw attention to the intensive work carried out in Europe to create a single electricity market, i.e. to bring together markets - the working name of such activities is Market Coupling - which would concern both the day-to-day market and the day-to-day market. As a result, electricity prices on wholesale markets would align to the same level in the Member States in such a way that the equilibrium price on this market would be a price for markets which are so combined. However, large countries, such as Poland, do not want to make the economy dependent [[♪ The bell ♪]] Only from imported energy. Poland also focuses on building its own sources of production, as this ensures energy security and the possibility of avoiding the risks arising from electricity imports.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u81	I ask the Minister to respond.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u82	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Indeed, the situation was difficult this year. It was difficult for students, it was difficult for teachers, especially those who got involved in the process of taking and conducting exams in the eighth grade. The situation was also difficult due to Matura, but the use of so-called. the prom law, i.e. the rules that allowed the director to classify, allowed the students, and there were more than 15 thousand of them. in 197 schools, get grades, be classified and therefore be able to take Matura. On the other hand, following a detailed answer to the question, it is necessary to recall a few important legal provisions that speak about what results and how evaluation is organised in Polish schools. Thus, people with a certificate of upper secondary or upper secondary education, i.e. general secondary school, technical school, second-cycle professional school, i.e. those with upper secondary or upper secondary education, can take part in the exam. The decision to take or not to take the examination shall be the responsibility of the person concerned. On the other hand, the exam cannot be taken by a person who, as a result of the final classification, did not receive positive assessments from all compulsory educational activities, i.e., in other words, did not graduate from school. These principles of assessment, classification and promotion of pupils are defined by three documents: the Education System Act and the implementing rules, i.e. the regulation and the statute of the school which defines what is called internal evaluation. It is therefore worth recalling that this assessment and classification falls within the competence of the teacher who conducts educational activities. At the beginning of the school year, teachers are obliged, which is important, to inform pupils and their parents of the educational requirements necessary for the student to receive annual classification assessments of the educational activities resulting from the teacher's curriculum. Before the annual meeting of the pedagogical board, the teachers shall inform the pupil and his parents of the annual classification assessment envisaged for him within the time limit and form specified in the school statute. A student, in particular those at risk of a negative assessment, may benefit from the possibility of increasing the envisaged annual assessment. It also contains a statute, or internal evaluation system, to inform about the mode of increase. If, and this is important, the teacher has not retained the procedure for determining the conditions for determining the annual assessment, the student or his or her parents may raise objections. The deadline for submitting these reservations shall cover the period from the date of the annual classification assessment to the expiry of 2 working days after the end of the teaching and education classes. The Director shall be required to set up a committee which shall carry out a test of the pupil's knowledge and skills in the event of irregularities concerning the determination of the annual classification assessment. In these circumstances, the assessment established by the committee shall become an annual classification assessment of the subject in question. And now what I have already said - in view of the situation of teacher strikes in April 2019, as a result of which not all pedagogical councils have carried out the activities resulting from their competences, we have introduced changes that have allowed this to be done. As a result, the students who graduated from high school were able to take the exam. I would like to say that the Ministry has not so far collected data on the number of students who have not taken the exam because they have not graduated from school, but because of the situation that is taking place, we will try to collect such data. And what we can say and what we will certainly do: we will examine all these situations, especially those in which complaints have been brought. Four complaints were received by the Ministry of National Education. Therefore, in accordance with its powers, the Ministry has forwarded these complaints to the curators for examination under pedagogical supervision. Each of these situations will be tested and analysed by the Ministry of Education. In other words, this situation is a new situation for us, but we managed to control it quickly, by updating the rules, though [[♪ The bell ♪]] it cannot be excluded that there have been cases where students feel injured. We will therefore examine each of these complaints. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u100	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! The environment is, of course, integrated with agriculture and rural areas, after all, the vast majority of Poland's territory is occupied by rural areas. The Ministry of the Environment recognises the problems that arise in the field of environmental protection, both in rural areas and, more precisely, in farmers. As far as the topics raised by the Member are concerned, a soil degradation programme has emerged. The liming program worked in previous years, unfortunately our predecessors, no longer remember when, they eliminated the funding of liming soil. We're restarting it. We're reinvigorating the soil degradation program by liming. In this programme we provide for the use of the allocation of funds at the level of PLN 300 million. We anticipate a subsidy for purchase of lime of PLN 300 per tonne of pure ingredient, if the holding is up to 25 ha. This is where we do gradation, so if the farms are in the range of 25.50 ha, then we donate 200 PLN to a ton of pure ingredient, and for farms in the range of 50.75 ha - 100 PLN to a ton of pure calcium ingredient. On the other hand, on farms above 75 ha, we no longer provide for such funding. Gradation has been taken into account because it is in small farms that soil degradation occurs most often and lime is the least used. The programme will be implemented by voivodship environmental and water management funds, but of course the cooperating authority will be the National Chemical and Agricultural Station. In fact, farmers should report to the National Chemical and Agricultural Station in order, first, to obtain soil testing in terms of acidity, second, to obtain a recommendation on the application of fertilising needs and to purchase this calcium fertiliser to distribute it and then to apply for a refund to the Regional Environmental Fund via the National Chemical and Agricultural Station. I hope that for a few years, we will be able to cyclically eliminate the problem of soil hyperacidity. The second problem is asbestos and this programme actually works. The scheme was probably established in 2003 and provided for the elimination of asbestos entirely by 2032. On the other hand, when I looked at the report of the Supreme Audit Chamber, which was announced in 2016, and assessed the implementation of this programme for the years 2009 and 2014, the Supreme Audit Office assessed the implementation of this programme absolutely negatively, indicating that if we acted at the rate at which asbestos was removed then, we would need another 200 years, not until 2032. I mean, like, you know, like, you know, like, you know, like, you know, you know, like, you know, you know, like, you know, like, like, you know, like, you know, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, like, Hence, the program, which is functioning, but to a very limited extent, we decided to speed up very much so that anyone who wants to change the roof cover and eliminate asbestos would have no problem with its development after taking it off the roof. That is why we have increased the financial allocation several times, initially to PLN 170 million. Of course, if there's more need, we'll increase it further. We have also made it easier for municipalities, because at the moment the aid for them is at 50%. Therefore, the municipalities do not seek to apply for these applications. We differentiate the aid, especially for small-income municipalities, i.e. less than 1.5 thousand. zł per person. When this G indicator is less than 1.5 thousand, the municipality receives 100% costs for the removal of asbestos. For the G indicator between 1500 and 2000 - 70% grants received by the municipality, 30% added from its resources. And at the richest municipalities, where the G is above 2,000, that's 40%. grants. Hence the poorest communities have 100% , the richest little less, but by far more than was anticipated in the existing programmes, and the amount of allocation is far greater. This will allow anyone who requests funding for the costs of removal of asbestos, the development of this, removal from the roof, transport, money for this. Unfortunately, this has broken down and, therefore, it is necessary for farmers to implement a programme for collecting these waste which is missing from farmers. We do not want them to go to illegal landfills or anywhere else, hence to recognize the problem, we are currently implementing a pilot programme in seven municipalities in the north of the Mazowieckie voivodeship, in those municipalities where there is intensive milk production and the problem is very strong. Once this pilot programme has been implemented, we will launch a programme covering Poland as a whole. We assume that the costs of collecting and developing film should not exceed 500 PLN/t, string and other waste - 700 PLN/t. That's what goes into rural areas, especially the volunteer fire department. We have increased this funding many times. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u170	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report to the Joint Commission: Education, Science and Youth, and the Territorial Government and Regional Policy regarding the parliamentary draft law amending the Teacher Charter Act and some other laws contained in prints No 3496 and 3523. Joined committees at their meeting on 12 June this year. they considered the bill mentioned above. A total of 11 amendments were tabled during the work: 7 substantive, 4 editorial and legislative amendments. The committees have accepted one substantive amendment and all editorial and legislative amendments and are asking the High Sejm to adopt a draft law from print No 3523. Which is worth noting, it is a unanimous recommendation. 42 Members voted in favour, there were no votes against or abstentions, which is to say 42 to 0. [[Applause]] There is no reason to be surprised, because the solutions contained in the project are very beneficial for teachers. As the applicants stressed, the draft law is the fulfilment of the provisions of the agreement between the government and representatives of the National Section for Education and Education of the NSZZ.Solidarity, which was signed on 7 April this year. The most important issue of the amendment is, of course, the teacher's salary increase of 9.6%. which will be paid from 1 September this year. The applicants stressed that the budgets prepared by the Platform Government in 2013 and 2016 did not provide for any funding for increases for teachers and salaries in the budgetary sphere at the time. Only the Government of Law and Justice carried out the valorization of teachers' wages in 2017. For this purpose, funding in the educational part of the general subsidy of PLN 413 million was provided and a programme of wage increases for the years 2018 to 2020 was prepared. Two of these increases have already been received by teachers. Since 1 April 2018, average wages have increased by 5.35% and by a further 5% since January 2019. The next 5% increase has been planned since January 2020, but in order to meet the expectations of teachers, the provisions of the Teacher Charter are amended to allow it to accelerate and increase its amount from 5 to, as I said, 9.6%. The applicants indicated that teachers had never received higher increases than in 2019. There's never been a 15% raise in one year. It is worth to add that in total over the last 2 years the teacher's salaries will increase by more than 21% and after the implementation of the September increase the basic salary of the teacher will evolve as follows: the trainee's teacher will receive PLN 2782, the contract teacher - PLN 2862, the teacher appointed - PLN 3250 and the teacher certified - PLN 3817. As far as average wages are concerned, the trainee's teacher will receive PLN 3338, the contract teacher - PLN 3705, the teacher appointed - PLN 4806 and the diplomat teacher - PLN 6141. The appropriations for the implementation of the increases shall be provided in the Budget Act. Another issue that has been taken up in the novel is the introduction of a new benefit for trainees' teachers, a start-up benefit of 1 000. zł. Young teachers, starting a job, will receive social support paid until 30 September of the year in which the teacher started his internship as a contract teacher. The condition for receiving this benefit will be employment of a minimum of 1/2 compulsory classes in one school or several schools combined. The financial consequences of the change are estimated at about PLN 28 million per year by the applicants. The payment of the benefit will be the task of the local government's own units. This is why a change has been made to the Teacher's Charter by providing guarantees for this new task. The distribution of the educational part of the general subsidy will take into account the funding of the start-up benefit, while the Income Act of the local government of the applicant proposes a change in the criteria for the distribution of the education subsidy in order to finance this benefit also for teachers of pre-school education institutions. Another important change is the introduction in the Teacher's Charter that the supplement for teachers of classes must not be less than 300 PLN. At the same time, the statutory possibility was provided for schools which are local self-government units to be able to set a higher amount of this allowance in the remuneration rules. At present, the legal situation is that the amount of the allowance for teachers of educators is determined by local self-government units in the remuneration regulations and the amount of these allowances varies greatly. Nearly 100,000. teachers, about 34%, receive allowances of less than PLN 100, another 155 thousand. teachers, i.e. around 55% , receives allowances between PLN 100 and PLN 200, and only 3% of teachers receive allowances higher than PLN 300. The average is approx. 128 PLN. I think that, therefore, teachers will appreciate the change that we are making, because in fact this 300 PLN is the amount that will, I think, motivate class teachers to get involved in the educational problems of young people. Another important change meeting the expectations of teachers is the shortening of the career promotion path and the return to evaluation of work before 1 September 2018. The mandatory assessment of teachers' work and the provision authorising the Minister of National Education to issue a regulation setting out detailed criteria for evaluation of work shall be abolished. This solution has been strongly criticised by teachers, and therefore the criteria for assessing the work will continue to be set out in the Teacher's Charter. There will also be a separation of the evaluation of the work from the promotion of the profession and the restoration of the evaluation of the teacher's professional achievements for the period of internship. In order to meet the expectations of teachers, it is also necessary to reduce the traineeship to the degree of contract teacher by 1 year, i.e. to 9 months, and to reduce the periods of school work necessary to start the course for the next degree of professional promotion: in the case of an internship to the degree of teacher appointed from 3 years to 2 years, in the case of an internship to the degree of teacher qualified from 4 years to 1 year. In addition, the examination before the examination committee for the degree of contract teacher will be replaced by the interview carried out by the selection committee. I am convinced that the solutions presented will be well accepted by the teaching community, because the amendment is another step towards increasing the prestige of the teaching profession, is another step towards improving the quality of Polish education. At the same time, applicants stressed that they were aware that the project did not meet all the expectations of teachers and that work was already underway on changes in the coming years, including wage increases. Therefore, as I said at the outset, on behalf of the Joint Committees, I would like the High Sejm to adopt the bill in print 3523. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u171	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Ms Teresa Wargock, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u172	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to address in general the issues that this law regulates. The reform of the education system started on 1 September 2017 is in line with the legal assumptions. We have also introduced provisions that have allowed us to continue our career if it turns out that the teacher has less than 1/2 jobs. Education reform has had a positive impact on the situation of teachers. Between 2017 and 2018 28 thousand arrived. employment and the number of teachers who are not employed has decreased. The organisational reform of the education system was accompanied by concern for the level of teachers' salaries. In 2017, for the first time in 5 years, the valorisation of teachers' salaries has been paid and the average salary has increased by 5.35% since April 2018. Another 5% increase was planned for January 2019 and January 2020. As you know, the increase planned for 2020 will be paid from 1 September 2019. Within 15 months, teachers' salaries will increase by 21%. And so the average salary of the diplomat is 60%. qualified teachers, will amount to PLN 6141. High Izbo! We proceed with the examination of item 21 of the agenda: the report of the Committee on Environmental Protection, Natural Resources and Forestry on the governmental draft law amending the Act on Nuclear Law and the Fire Protection Act (prints No. 3237 and 3503). I ask Mrs Anna Paluch to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u226	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I present a report on the Government's draft law amending the Act on Nuclear Law and the Fire Protection Act. The draft is contained in print No 3237 and the committee's report in print No 3503. The project was addressed to first reading on 20 February and its first reading in the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry took place on 12 March. Following detailed work on the project, the Commission shall first submit a report in its special sub-committee, and thereafter in full composition. It should be noted that the deadline for notification had not expired for this print project 3237 at the start of parliamentary work. In anticipation of the final position of the European Commission, we have therefore fired work in the subcommittee so that the final report of the subcommittee can be adopted after taking into account any comments resulting from the notification. These, however, did not come in. The subcommittee report was adopted on 15 May and the committee report on 11 June. The report of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry was adopted by 13 votes in favour, with three abstentions and no votes against. High Izbo! This amendment aims to ensure the highest possible level of nuclear safety and radiological protection in Poland in order to adapt this level to European and global standards. The Act implements Council Directive 2013/59/Euratom of 5 December 2013 establishing basic safety standards for protection against risks arising from exposure to ionising radiation, which repeals the five previous Directives. The implementation of Directive 2013/59 responds to problems which have not yet been adequately addressed in Polish law. In particular, the aim is to ensure more effective regulatory control of activities related to exposure to ionising radiation, introduction of dose limiters for ionising radiation, regulation of non-medical imaging of persons from the general public, changes in the limit values for workers in relation to new results of studies on the effects of ionising radiation on the body. Further problems include increasing the protection of external workers against ionising radiation, clarifying the responsibilities and powers of those exercising internal supervision of activities, increasing the transparency of the activities of nuclear surveillance authorities by making a surveillance control programme available to the public. Increased awareness of the possibility of contacting an uncontrolled source, informing about the actions to be taken in such a situation, and training officers of relevant services who may encounter such radiation sources. Another issue is the protection against exposure to ionising radiation caused by radon in workplaces, buildings, premises and rooms intended for people's stay, which has not yet been regulated in Polish law. The next issue is to increase the safety of the use of ionising radiation for medical purposes and to ensure protection against exposure related to naturally occurring radioactive substances, including substances related to mining activities. High Izbo! The second Directive 2014/87/Euratom of 8 July 2014 amending Directive 71 of 2009 was implemented in this project following the disaster of the Fukushima nuclear power plant. It aims to establish a Community framework for the nuclear safety of nuclear installations. Its implementation will help prepare for the possibility of major accidents in European nuclear facilities and address nuclear safety problems, among others. as regards: the introduction of an obligation for an undertaking which carries out activities consisting in the construction, commissioning, operation or decommissioning of a nuclear installation to ensure that its subcontractors and suppliers maintain personnel resources at a level suitable for carrying out tasks for that entity. This also applies to the extent to which an entity is responsible for the activities of suppliers and subcontractors. Another issue is the introduction of an obligation to use in the design, location, construction, commissioning, operation, including repairs and modernisation, and the elimination of technical and organisational solutions which will ensure the prevention of accidents at all stages of the operation of a nuclear facility and, in the event of an accident, reduce the effects and avoid early or large radioactive releases. Another issue is the introduction of management systems that will give the right priority to nuclear safety, facilitate the verification of safety principles and practices and the timely reporting of safety concerns by staff. Very often it is that workers notice certain things, so they must be able to report matters that raise their doubts. And all such remarks and signals should be considered with the utmost care within the responsible entity, because such signals often avoid the threat which is tragic for the population of people exposed to nuclear activities. Another issue is the introduction of systematic and regular re-assessment of the safety of a nuclear facility and the introduction of periodic self-assessments and international peer reviews on selected aspects of the safety of nuclear installations. This is a very large group of issues which, I will remind you, are implementing Directive 87 of 2014. Other amendments made to this Act are based on the 2013 report of the IRRS, the International Atomic Energy Agency, and concern inter alia. rules for carrying out activities by entities from the European Union which provide services in this field, i.e. to the extent related to the occurrence of nuclear radiation, in Poland. Another issue is the comprehensive regulation of planning and emergency procedures and the introduction of a statutory mandate for the adoption by the Council of Ministers of a nuclear safety strategy. It is also a matter of introducing the grounds for dismissing the President of the Agency and the term of office of the President of the Agency into the Act. Of course, we're talking about an atomic energy agency, I'm sorry about the State Atomic Agency. There is also a revision of the rules on the waste fund and a revision of the rules on the physical protection of nuclear installations. High Izbo! During the work of the subcommittee, five amendments were made to the draft at the commission stage and eight earlier at the subcommittee stage. The law is, of course, an extremely extensive law. It is difficult here to discuss all the issues raised in detail. The length of work in the committee, and earlier in the subcommittee, shows how complicated this matter is, how diverse it is, how many areas it concerns and how many issues it regulates. Let me just say this: five annexes, 193 pages of the text of the bill, that's probably one of the major laws we've been working on. I would also like to talk about the last point, namely the article. 39th Act, which regulates its entry into force. The law shall enter into force within 30 days of the date of its publication, with the exception of Article. 2. Art. 2 concerns amendments to the Fire Protection Act, i.e. the inclusion of a national fire-fighting system in the fight against nuclear risks. The new features of the Fire Protection Act will enter into force on 1 January next year. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I would like to ask that the draft law contained in the committee's report in print No 3503 be adopted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u227	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Krzysztof Kubów, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u228	Dear Marshal, Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club as regards the government draft law amending the Act on Nuclear Law and the Fire Protection Act, in print no. 3237 and 3503. The amendment aims to implement into national law the provisions of the BSS Directive establishing basic safety standards to protect against the risks arising from exposure to ionising radiation and the NSD Directive establishing a Community framework for nuclear safety, nuclear installations, and to make other legal changes with regard to nuclear safety and radiological protection resulting from the evaluation of existing legislation. The most important solutions are to protect against exposure to ionising radiation caused by radon in workplaces, buildings, premises and other spaces intended for humans, to change the limit values for workers due to new results of studies on the effects of ionising radiation on the human body, or to introduce limiters for ionising radiation doses. The changes also concern increasing the protection against ionising radiation of external workers or increasing the transparency of the activities of nuclear surveillance authorities by making the surveillance programme available to the public and increasing the information to be taken in situations of contact with an uncontrolled source, i.e. an abandoned or missing radioactive source, as well as training officers of relevant services who may encounter such sources. The amendment also introduces protection against exposure to naturally occurring radioactive substances, including those associated with mining activities. In the case of the Fire Protection Act, the amendment concerns the content of the article. 14 by adding paragraph. 1b with the following content: The national fire-fighting system shall be involved in the eradication of the threat, including rescue operations, in the event of a radiation incident. In this way, the tasks of the national fire-fighting system will be extended to contribute to the eradication of hazards, including rescue operations, in the event of a radiation incident. In the current state of law, there was no service intended to act in the event of a radiation incident, and so far the actual preparation of the units of the State Fire Service had no statutory authority. The proposed wording of the provision will allow for precise identification by the Chief of the State Fire Service of tasks, responsibilities and rescue procedures in the event of the involvement of the forces and resources of the State Fire Department during the operation on-site of the radiation incident. The measures put in place will ensure the highest level of radiological protection for workers, patients and the general public. This is a very good project, which is why on behalf of the Law and Justice Club I ask the High House to adopt this bill. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Ionizing radiation may pose a particular risk to human life and health. The safety of both staff and patients is important. The exposure activities are divided into several categories. Category 1 covers activities related to exposure to radiation events on the premises of an organizational unit. The second category covers activities occurring within the unit, but with exposure of the general public to stochastic effects of radiation. I'm not going to discuss this in detail because there's little time, and it's all in the bill. Category 3 includes only events related to the exposure of an entity. This also applies to students, students, trainees of medical or technical universities. Ladies and gentlemen, these doses have been determined for employees, students, students and apprentices. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u246	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The project is about speeding up the process of eliminating the risks associated with the presence of non-normative concentrations of certain air pollutants in most areas of Poland and implementing the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union. Poland, like other European countries, has problems meeting air quality requirements, which results, among others. from the fact that the overruns are generated by the social and communal sector and transport. To date, the assessment of the level of substances in the air and the classification of zones, as defined by the resolution of the voivodship sejmiks, had 18 months from the date of receipt of the results provided by the voivodship environmental inspectors. The project foresees that the time limit for preparing the air protection programme will be 3 months shorter. The programmes will be implemented after receiving an assessment of air quality for 2018, and the deadline for their adoption by the voivodship sejm is 15 June 2020. This was agreed with the European Commission, which committed Poland to present by June 2020 an air protection programme meeting the requirements of the Directive and a judgment of the Court of Justice. In order to improve the effectiveness of the implementation of the air protection programme, the management of voivodships introduced an obligation to monitor the annual progress of corrective actions and short-term action plans in municipalities. Changes in the air quality management system by the local government and changes in the scope of the air protection programme will result in the preparation of documentation and reporting, and will allow for the transfer of monitoring of existing corrective actions from the level of the municipality to the level of the voivodship. The information obtained about the operation of the air protection programme and the short-term action plans will enable us to fulfil our country's reporting obligation to the European Commission. On the other hand, the recommendations of the Supreme Audit Chamber require continuous cooperation with other actors and ongoing, consistent and systematic analysis of the effectiveness of corrective actions and the extent to which the objectives of the air protection programme are implemented in the regions. The amendments aim to discipline the authorities drawing up the air protection programme so that the actions envisaged in the programme allow the actual reduction of exceedance of the limit values as soon as possible in the individual regions of the countries concerned. These changes are in line with European Union law. They are needed to monitor air quality to eliminate air pollution risks. The Law and Justice Club welcomes these legislative changes. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u258	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Thank you very much for the positions of clubs, for what, among other things. Mrs Chybick and Mr Roletsky have said about this work. As far as club positions are concerned, there is a great deal of common concern about air quality. There were also many questions which actually question the division of power in Poland and the way in which public space is organized. If we are discussing why it is not the minister and not the government that impose certain solutions on local governments, then we are going back to the very beginnings of decentralisation in Poland, the role of local governments, as well as the role of central institutions. Mrs Lenartowicz, speaking of speeding up the process, was gracious to quote the introduction to the justification of this law. This law is really about speeding up the process, and if we're talking about the process, we need to define it. It's hard to accelerate something that doesn't exist. Therefore, if we have a trial, we must have deadlines. This law, on the other hand, also introduces standards. In other words, we will be able to analyse how individual commitments are implemented by local governments, which will accept them themselves, because that is the division of power in Poland. We cannot therefore contest the way in which this law is designed without going back to discussing the role of central authority in relation to local authority. The tightening of the corset, as you would have suggested, would lead us towards local governments, I think, also restricting the freedom of local governments. However, the process that we are introducing and which we are speeding up gives freedom to decide at the level of the local government, freedom to choose resources, freedom to choose the sources of funding that the local government considers most appropriate to respond to the challenges it is facing when it comes to clean air. Let us remind you that the previous transposition of this law did not lead to a speeding up of the process, because it also did not define something that is very important at this point in time from the point of view of the implementation of the judgment of the TSUE, i.e. the shortest possible deadlines. Let us finally remind you that this law does not introduce any additional financial penalties. So far, the voivodship WIÓS also had such prerogatives as to impose financial penalties, but since the process was defined in a fragmented way, there was no possibility to monitor progress, these penalties were arbitrary. In a situation where the local government itself defines how it wants to carry out its obligations, how it wants to fight air pollution in its territory, and then defines its own plan, it only allows for some progress to be made - and this is also the role of voivodships: monitoring progress. On the other hand, it is not the voivodship, so not the central instance that imposes penalties, but THE MOST. Therefore, it seems to me that here we cannot logically question this law, without undermining the division of power in Poland and without undermining the process of decentralisation, which, as far as air protection issues are concerned, has been dated since at least 2008, when these prerogatives were transferred to local governments. It seems to me, then, that as far as these aspects are concerned, we can agree that this law accelerates the process that it redefines this process. Acceleration is also based on the element that has been criticised in this Act, namely the fact that we limit the preparation of air protection programmes and short-term action plans by 3 months. With regard to the measures that are being taken at government level, there have also been such questions - I think it is difficult to disagree with the fact that investing PLN 103 billion in the clean air programme is an absolutely unprecedented solution. It is the first time that this programme has shaped, reflected real concern and really mobilises huge state resources to address the challenges of air pollution in a concrete way. So far, almost 60,000. applications were addressed to the National Environment Fund, several thousand contracts were signed. I will also answer Mr Protas' question: the first amounts have already been paid. It is not true, therefore, that not a single dollar has gone to the Poles. The first amounts have already been paid. What's more, we already have 60,000 in this today. applications addressed to the national fund the amount of grants at the level of PLN 1 billion, so this is a huge amount for which during these 8 months there was demand from Poles. Let us remind you that the entire 10-year programme is to mobilise the sum of PLN 103 billion. As regards the sources of funding on the part of local governments, they are not news. This has been the case so far. It is at this point that it is an incentive for local authorities to take advantage of the broad spectrum of funding sources to combat air pollution. We have here national environmental funds, but we also encourage you to make greater use of such sources of funding, such as PO IiŚ. With regard to short-term action plans, as regards the interaction between the mayor, the mayor, the president of the city and the relevant councils or seymists, this is also the case in which the question of the division of power that this law has shown is revealed. It is up to the governor, mayor or president of the city to define his own consultation process, which he considers most appropriate to oblige his local self-government unit in relation to its constituents to local activists. In this context, it is also necessary to read the possibility or provision of this law. This short-term action plan is in no way covered by the corset as to how local authorities will accept it. Finally, as far as the point at the end was concerned, which you were pleased to ask about the levels of alerting and information, this is a project on which work is being carried out in parallel with this law, a draft regulation of the Minister of the Environment. The current 2012 indicators of 200 and 300 are at this point being intensively consulted with the Minister of Health in such a spirit as to provide the best available knowledge at central level and reflect environmental concerns through the most ambitious alarming and information indicators. The work is therefore carried out in parallel with the work on the law. I hope that's all, Marshal, when it comes to my answers. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u264	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I will present my position on the report of the committee on the government draft law amending the Act on Environmental Protection and the Act on Crisis Management. The project envisages accelerating the elimination of risks associated with the presence of supra-normative concentrations of certain air pollutants. This includes: exceed the permitted levels of PM10 and PM2,5 particulate matter, as well as nitrogen dioxide and the target benzo (a) pyrene. The new rules implement the judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of 22 February 2018. It concerns the observed levels of PM10 particulate matter (average daily) in 35 zones and PM10 particulate matter (average annual) in 9 zones (out of 46 zones in the country). Poland, like most European Union countries, has difficulty meeting air quality requirements due to PM10 particulate matter pollution. These overruns are particularly responsible for the social and communal sectors and for transport. The basis for corrective action is the resolution of the Sejm of the Voivodship on the Air Protection Programme (POP). Currently, the voivodship sejmik - within 18 months from the date of receipt of the results carried out by the voivodship environmental inspectors assessing the levels of substances in the air and classification of zones - defines the POP in the resolution. The PSL-UED Club will vote in favour of the project.

To overcome everything that weakens her and destroys her - everything that does not allow her to be a true environment of life and love. He was and is a signpost for us that we can probably walk. His actions were an expression of extraordinary courage. In 1981, he survived an assassination attempt on his life. On 2 April 2005, with great pain we said goodbye to our beloved Pope, who went to the Father's House and, we believe, intercedes for us and blesses us from heaven. By this resolution, the Polish Parliament expresses its gratitude and pays tribute to the Great Pope. John Paul II, who taught us openness, understanding, but also courage in defending the Christian values on which Poland and Europe are based. ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u282	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Culture and Media on the draft resolution on the establishment of the year 2020 by the Year of Hetman Stanisław Żółkiewski, print No 3418. It was a parliamentary draft resolution presented by the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. The Marshal of the Sejm sent him to the Committee on Culture and Media for first reading. The Committee on Culture and Media at first reading considered this draft resolution at its meeting on 15 May 2019. In the course of the work, there were minor amendments tabled in accordance with the joint work of Members of the Committee on Culture and Media, and valuable comments were made at the initiative of the Legislative Office. One thing that can be interpreted differently is the question of the number of Polish troops, as well as the Moscow army, in the battle of Kłuszyn. We have adopted such a number here, which results from the latest findings of the researcher Radosław Sikora - a historian who specializes in the history not only of the Kuszyn Battle, but also of the Husaria in general. According to his latest research, Polish forces were few. It was according to various studies up to 3,800 soldiers, but this is more formal than actual. In fact, even 2700 soldiers, of course mostly Husaria, could be there according to him. I am talking about this because in many previous publications there is even talk about 7 thousand when it comes to Polish troops. As far as the Moscow forces are concerned, they were essentially combined Moscow-Swedish forces. There were about four and a half thousand. Swedes and, of course, many more Moscows. This is why we are talking about more than 30 thousand Moscow forces, whose numbers, including rolling stock, could reach up to 40,000, although there could be fewer on this first battle line - about 18 thousand. Anyway, I don't think it's the most important thing here, but because this issue was being addressed, I referred to it. The most important thing is that hetman Stanisław Żółkiewski is indeed one of the most outstanding leaders in the history of Polish weapons, and given that he was the creator of the Kuszynska Victoria, that he was the conqueror of Moscow, who knows, maybe he was the most outstanding leader in Polish history. Therefore, the Committee on Culture and Media requests the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft resolution. I will read the text of the draft resolution after work within the Committee on Culture and Media: The resolution on the establishment of the year 2020 by the Year of Hetman Stanisław Żółkiewski. Among the characters included among the most outstanding leaders in the history of Polish weapons is the figure of the hetman Stanisław Żółkiewski. Stanisław Żółkiewski coat of arms Lubicz was born in 1547 in Turynka near Lviv in a wealthy noble family. His father later became a Russian voivode. Stanisław Żółkiewski participated in all wars fought by the Republic in the second half of the 16th century and in the beginning of the 17th century. First he fought alongside the experienced leader and Chancellor Jan Zamoyski, participated in the expeditions against Habsburgs and Moscow, Moldova and Voloszczyznia, then he won in many campaigns against Russia, Sweden, the Cossacks, Turkey and the Tatars. He served a number of the most important offices of the First Republic: he was king's secretary, field captain, Lviv chestellan, Kiev voivode, and at the end he was a great crown captain and great crown chancellor. In 1609 he headed the Polish troops in the next Polish-Russian war and began the siege of Smoleńsk. On July 4, 1610, with only about 3,000 rides at his disposal, especially the Husaria, he defeated over 30 thousand combined Russian and Swedish troops under Kłuszyn. On 28 August, after his victory in Moscow, he signed a deal with the Russian boyars, under which the Polish king Władysław was declared the tsar of Russia. Unfortunately, King Sigmund III Vasa did not accept this agreement. On 29 October 1611 Stanisław Żółkiewski brought to Warsaw the deposed Tsar Wasyl Szujski and his two brothers who paid tribute to the Polish king, the so-called. The Russian platoon. He always put the good of Poland above his own benefits, stood faithfully on the side of successive kings, also in internal disputes, despite the critical opinion about the policy of Zygmunt III Vasa. He advocated religious tolerance and conflict relief. Stanisław Żółkiewski was the author of an outstanding work of journalism: 'The beginning and progress of the Moscow War', also known as 'The History of the Moscow War until Smoleńsk's reign. Stanisław Żółkiewski died on October 6, 1620 at Mohylow nad Dniestr, defending the Republic of Poland against the Turks in reverse fights from Cecory. He refused to abandon his comrades and to escape. His attitude in the last moments of life is tied to the maxim: 'It is sweet and honorable to die for Homeland'. He was buried in the crypt of the church in Żółkwi, the city he himself founded. The inscription on the tombstone says, "One day our avenger will rise from the bones." His great-grandson was John III Sobieski. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, recognizing the great merits of Stanisław Żółkiewski, the creator of the Kuszyn Victoria and the conqueror of Moscow, the persistent defender of the Homeland, for whom he gave his life, establishes the year 2020 in the 400th anniversary of his death with the Year of Hetman Stanisław Żółkiewski. As I said, the Committee on Culture and Media recommends that the High House adopt this draft resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on the parliamentary draft resolution on the establishment of the year 2020 by the Year of Hetman Stanisław Żółkiewski. Stanisław Żółkiewski coat of arms Lubicz was one of the most outstanding politicians and commanders of the First Republic. He was born in 1547 as the son of Stanisław and Zofia Lipska from Goraj. Like most noble children, he studied at home and then at cathedral school. After their completion, he developed his own knowledge, reaching an intellectual level that exceeded the average intellectual level of many of his contemporaries. He loved reading Horace, Cicero and Virgil, Kochanowski, Skarga, studied the memoirs of Caesar and the Council of War of Ternowski. Since 1566 he has worked with Jan Zamoyski on internal policy and has accompanied him in wars with Moscow, Moldova, Wołoszczyzna and Sweden. Being faithful to the election kings of Stefan Batore and Zygmunt III Vazie, he gained further military promotions. He became the captain of the field, and then the captain of the great crown. When Zygmunt III appointed him Chancellor of the Grand Crown in 1618, he became the first person after the King. Stanisław Żółkiewski showed personal courage when he obtained war grinds. Battle baptism took place in 1577 near Lubieszów. Then in the years 1579 and 1582 he participated in the war against Moscow for Inflanty, bravely fighting both in the field and during the siege of Pskov. During the reignless period, he contributed to the victory at the Bull. The largest military achievement of Stanisław Żółkiewski remains the Moscow expedition and the victory at Kłuszyn in 1610. Like Caesar's tactics in the battle of Farsalos, he defeated several times the combined Moscow and Swedish forces. After the occupation of Moscow, leading reasonable negotiations with the boyars, led to the actual election of Prince Władysław to the Moscow throne. At the same time, he enslaved Tsar Wasyl Szujski and his two brothers. Despite his deteriorating health, he took care of the defense of the Eastern Endlands threatened by the Turks, Tatars and Moldovans. In 1620 Stanisław Żółkiewski went with the army to the camp near Cecora in Moldova, where the heroic death of a soldier, attacked by the superior enemy, was killed at the time of his return to Poland. The fame of the hetman was initiated thanks to the magnificent speech by Tomasz Zamoyski in the Seym on December 5, 1620, which was then printed widely throughout the country, establishing the legend of uncompromising commander and politician. Therefore, taking into account the life and service of Stanisław Żółkiewski to the king and the Republic of Poland, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the adoption by the Sejm of a resolution establishing the year 2020 by the Year of Hetman Stanisław Żółkiewski. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The Committee on Culture and Media, after first reading and considering this draft resolution at its meeting on 15 May 2019, adopted it without objection and asks the High Sejm to adopt a draft resolution with the following wording: The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the establishment of the year 2020 by the Year of the Battle of Warsaw. In 1918, Poland regained its independence after a long time of captivity. However, it was not given to Poles to enjoy the room. The young country had to face the ruthless machine of Bolshevik Russia. 'By the body of White Poland' was supposed to lead 'way to a global fire', as the Red Marshal Mikhail Tuchaczewski said. A decisive battle of the Polish-Bolshevik war took place 100 years ago, on August 13 and 15, 1920. The heroic Polish Army led by Marshal Józef Piłsudski, supported among others by the Chief of General Staff - General Tadeusz Jordan-Rozwadowski, defended Poland's independence. Victory has also crossed out plans to extend the Bolshevik Revolution to Western Europe. According to Lord Edgar D.Abernon, this was 18. A breakthrough battle in the history of the world. 'If Charles the Hammer hadn't stopped the Saracen invasion of Tours, the Koran would have been taught today in schools in Oxford. If Piłsudski and Weygand had failed to stop the triumphal march of the Red Army near Warsaw, this would have resulted not only in a dangerous turn in the history of Christianity, but also in the fundamental threat of the entire Western civilization," wrote D.Abernon. August 15, 1920 was the day of victory of all Poles. The Church, which mobilized the entire nation to defend itself against an invader from the East, played a huge role in those days. The attitude of the papal archbishop's nuncio grew to the symbol's rank. Achille Ratti, later Pope Pius XI, who did not leave Warsaw in those hot August days, and the character of the heroic priest Fr. Ignacy Skorupka, who died in the battle of Ossow. This unification of Poles in those days was a true 'Wonder of the Vistula'. The President of the Republic of Poland Prof. Lech Kaczyński, on the eve of the Polish Army Day in 2009 said: 'In these August days the fate of our country, but also of the entire continent, was decided. It was a moment of great national rehearsal, which the Poles passed victoriously. [...] Her heroes, the real creators of the victory, were soldiers of the Polish Army who fought for everything. Finally, the solidarity and sacrifice of the whole society, which at this dramatic moment had abandoned disputes and divisions. A great, wonderful lesson on August 15, 1920 should always be valid and informative for us." The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, convinced of the special importance of the Battle of Warsaw in order to preserve Poland's independence and stop the Bolshevik attacks on Western Europe, establishes the year 2020 as the Year of the Battle of Warsaw. Thank you for your attention and I would like to ask Members to adopt this resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u312	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the parliamentary club Civic Platform on the establishment of the year 2020 with Roman Ingarden. Roman Witold Ingarden is a Polish philosopher, a phenomenonologist with very rich achievements. He was a student of Edmund Husserl and studied philosophy, especially ontological, axiological and aesthetic issues. He was born in Krakow, in a rich family and already as a student he was passionate about literature, music, but also sports. He studied philosophy in Göttingen, mathematics and psychology in Vienna and Lviv. He returned to Poland after defending his doctorate. During World War II, he joined the Lviv Underground University. At that time, teaching and research were in the philosopher's eyes an expression of patriotism and an attempt to save the sense of life. In 1946 he received the title of professor of the ordinary Jan Kazimierz University in Lviv. After the German occupation he taught at the University of Toruń and the Jagiellonian University in Krakow. In 1950, he was removed from work at the university because the communist authorities then found his work incompatible with the existing ideological priorities. In 1957, however, he returned to the Jagiellonian University as head of the Philosophy Department and worked there until 1963. On 14 June 1970, sudden death took him out of work. At the time, he was 77 years old. Ingarden gained fame and recognition because of his considerations. He enjoyed the opinion of one of the most important Polish philosophers. He was a member of the Polish Academy of Skills and the Polish Academy of Sciences. His greatest works are about literary works and about learning about literary works. He was recognized by American scientists as one of the most outstanding philosophers of modern times, and in Germany as a leading representative of phenomenology and as a classic of philosophy. He published a total of 224 papers. Many have been translated into foreign languages. He left a wealthy handicraft legacy. Given the above, the Civic Platform parliamentary club is in favour of adopting a resolution [[♪ The bell ♪]] on the establishment of the year 2020 by Roman Ingarden. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-06-13-sejm-82-2.u314	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Dear Young People at the Gallery! I present on behalf of the Kukiz Club15 my position on the resolution related to the 50th anniversary of Roman Ingarden's death. Roman Ingarden, one of the most outstanding Polish philosophers of phenomenologists, shaping the thinking of many generations of Poles, deserves to be fully remembered. The doctorate obtained from Edmund Husserl did not close Roman Ingarden's vision of the world only as part of Husserlow's theory. On the contrary, he took a broad polemic approach with the views of his master and their criticism. In a rational position, however, Ingarden did not reject other points of view. He was a model of a scientist for whom academic discourse based on well-founded assumptions is more important than stubborn, obstinate conduct at the same time. After leaving the phenomenology, in time he formed his own philosophy school, with a wide group of supporters, to the extent of its inner sovereignty, contrary to the enslaved reality around it. The views presented allowed Roman Ingarden to confront the extremely unfavorable dialogue and development of the intellect of times with the fundamental subjects for human existence. The great proof of this is his monumental work, the dispute about the existence of the world, written during the darkest night of the German occupation, as well as the translation of the "Critique of Pure Reason" by Immanuel Kant, which was performed during the period of deprivation of Ingarden's right to lecture at the Jagiellonian University. Ingarden's scientific achievements are impressive, but much more important is the consequence presented by Ingarden in terms of attitudes, choices and faithfulness to principles. He showed this fidelity during the years of the German-Soviet occupation when in Lviv he joined the activities of secret secondary and university education. Ingarden did not collaborate with the occupiers in any form. After the end of World War II, despite the awareness of the Soviet and communist occupations and the reality of the ever-increasing Marxist ideology in Polish universities, he remained faithful to his views and did a devastating criticism of Marx's theory, for which he paid the 6-year ban on teaching. The correspondence with Edith Stein certainly influenced her views and attitude. She saw the responsibilities of the man of science, but above all ethical principles in a manner similar to Ingarden. In turn Charles Wojtyła or Józef Tischner are examples of Ingarden's disciples who, without even identifying themselves with some of the views of their master, [[♪ The bell ♪]] They took from him elements of the art of discourse, causing the necessary reflection on the presented thesis or theories. The Kukiz Club15 is in favour of a procedated resolution. Thank you. [[Applause]] [[President of the session includes Deputy Marshal Barbara Dolniak Sejm]]
